:دیکشنری انگلیسی به انگلیسی

  والیــــت

 استاد الهه پیر یــابی–

ـ ر بــابــابی

  استاد امــــید ش  یـــــعت

   توجه مهم 1
 : 
  نکات که در 
 کتابهای دریس ذکر نشده اند، 
 مطالعه آنها برای زبان عمویم کنکور اختیاری بوده 
 و رصفأ در 
کنکور منحرصأ زبان 
 احتمال طرایح سوال از آنها یم رود، به 
 صورت "
)نکته (زبان تخصیص " مشخص شده 
 اند .
 
    
 
   توجه مهم 2
 : 
 هیچگاه حفظ لغات به تنهاتی نیم تواند در موفقیت ما در کنکور و یادگیر ی زبان
انگلییس مؤثر باشد  و 
رصفأ در 
 جمالت معنادار و استاندارد یم  توان به 
 کاربرد صحیح
واژگان 
 .مسلط شد "مکالمه"، "
 ریدینگ کتاب درش
و 
 کتاب
کار" درس به درس که در این جزوه با 
  معن 
 روان فاریس نوشته 
 ،اند
یم تواند برای 
  آشنات با کاربرد 
 لغات 
 .مفید باشند

،کنکوری از

آزمونهای آزماییس" و "
 تستهای سایت آزمون سازی  نتا م" 
 .استفاده شده است
 
 با کمال
افتخار 
 منتظر "انتقادات " 
 و"
 پیشنهادات" 
 همگی شما دوستان
هستم .

Failure is a part of life.
 
If you don’t fail, you won’t learn. 
 
If you don’t learn, you will never succeed.

“Persian Meanings” 
“Words” 
“Persian Meanings” 
ability 
  تواناتی ، لیاقت، شایستگ 
completely 
 کامأل، به طور کامل 
absolutely 
 کامآل، قطعأ 
confident 
   مطمئ 
access 
 دسی  یس، دستیاتی - دسی  یس پیدا کردن 
consider 
   برریس کردن، در نظر گرفئ 
across 
 از عرض، در امتداد 
continent 
 قاره 
action اقدام، عمل ، کار 
create

Reading (Vision Two - Lesson One) 
(Student Book: Pages 24 - 25)      
 
 
 
 
“Languages of the world”
 "زبان
ـــــ
های دن
یا ــــ"
  
     
   
 
 
 
    Language is a system of communication. It uses written and spoken forms. People use language to 
communicate with each other in a society. They exchange knowledge, beliefs, wishes, and feelings 
through it. 
 
    
 زبان سیستم برقراری ارتباط است. آن از شکل های نوشتاری و گفتاری استفاده یم کند. مردم در جامعه از زبان استفاده یم 
 کنند تا با یکدیگر
ارتباط برقرار کنند. آن 
( ها از طریق آن
زبان
) تبادل دانش، عقاید، آرزوها و احساسات یم 
 .کنند 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
      Languages vary greatly from region to region. They are so different that a person may not understand 
the language of someone from another region, country or continent. It is not surprising to hear that today 
about 7000 languages exist in the world. There are more than 2000 languages in Africa, 1000 in the 
Americas, more than 2250 in Asia, about 230 in Europe, and more than 1300 in Oceania. 
 
    زبانها از یک منطقه به منطقه ی دیگر خییل فرق یم کنند. آن 
 ها آنقدر متفاوت هستند که یک شخص ممکن است زبان یک نفر از یک
منطقه، کشور یا قاره 
 ی دیگر را درک نکند. تعجب ندارد 
 که بشنویم امروزه حدود7000
 زبان در دنیا وجود دارند. در 
 آفریقا بیش از2000
 
(زبان،)
 
 در آمریکا1000
 (زبان )، در آسیا بیش از2250
 (زبان )، در اروپا حدود230
 (زبان
) و در 
 اقیانوسیه بیش از1300
 )زبان( 
 .وجود دارند 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
    Native speakers of these languages range in number from very large, with hundreds of millions of 
speakers, to very small, with fewer than 10 speakers. The most popular language in the world is Chinese. 
More than one billion people in the world speak Chinese. 
 
    
 صحبت کننده 
 های بویم این زبانها از نظر تعداد از خییل زیاد، با صدها میلیون صحبت کننده، تا خییل کم، با کم تر از10
 
 صحبت کننده
 .متغیر هستند. پرطرفدارترین زبان دنیا چین  است. بیش از یک میلیارد نفر در دنیا چین  صحبت یم کنند 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-----------------------------
--------
-------------------------------------
 
     Interestingly, English has fewer native speakers than Chinese, but there are about one billion learners 
of English all around the world. They learn English as an international language. 
 
    جالب این که ان
گلییس صحبت کننده 
 های بویم کم تری از چین  دارد اما در شتاش جهان حدود یک میلیارد فراگیر انگلییس وجود دارد. آنها
زبان انگلییس را به عنوان یک زبان بئر  الملیل یاد یم  
 .گیر ند 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
      About fifty percent of the world’s languages have fewer than 5000 speakers. In the beginning of the 
twenty-first century, 204 languages had fewer than 10 speakers and 344 languages had between 10 and 
99 speakers. The 548 languages with fewer than 99 speakers make up nearly 8 percent of the world’s 
languages. We call them ‘endangered languages. As the speakers of such languages grow old and die, 
their languages will die, too. 
 
    
 حدود50
 درصد از زبان 
 های دنیا کمی  از5000
 
 ،صحبت کننده دارند. در ابتدای این قرن204
 زبان کم 
 تر از10
 
 صحبت کننده و344
 
 زبان
  بئر10
 
 تا99
 
 .صحبت کننده داشتند548
 
 زبان با کمی  از99
 
 صحبت کننده حدود8 درصد زبان های دنیا را تشکیل یم 
" دهند. ما آنها را زبان 
های در معرض خطر
" یم نامیم. همچنان که صحبت کننده های چنئر  زبان هاتی پیر یم شوند و یم میر ند، زبان 
 .هایشان نیر  خواهند مرد 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
     All languages are really valuable, despite their differences. Every language is an amazing means of 
communication that meets the needs of its own speakers. It is impossible to imagine the world without 
language. Therefore, we should respect all languages, no matter how different they are and how many 
speakers they have. 
 
 
    همه ی زبانها عیل رغم تفاوت هایشان واقع أ باارزش هستند. هر زبات  یک وسیله ی ارتبایط شگفت 
 انگیر  است که نیازهای صحبت کننده 
هایش را بر آورده یم 
 کند. غیر ممکن است که دنیا را بدون
زبان تصور کرد. بنابراین، ما باید به همه 
 ی زبانها احی  ام بگذاریم، مهم نیست که آنها

(Workbook: Pages 10 - 11)      
 
 
 
“Hints for learning a language better”  
"
 نکاب  برای
یادگیر ی بهی  یک زبان"
  
 
ج      
    Learning a new language doesn’t always mean sitting in the classroom and studying language books. 
In fact, language teachers suggest that you do plenty of extra learning outside of school, places like your 
home or a library near you. There are a number of ways to improve your understanding of the language. 
For example, if you want to improve your English or any other foreign languages, you should consider 
some of these hints. If you add a few of these ideas to your day-to-day language learning, you’ll certainly 
see some improvement. 
    
 آ
موخئ   یک زبان تازه همیشه به معنای نشسئ   در کالس درس و مطالعه کتابهای زبان نیست. در واقع، معلم های زبان به شما 
 توصیه یم
کنند میر ان زیادی از آموخئ   اضاف  را در مکان 
 هاتی خارج از
مدرسه همانند خانه خود یا در یک کتابخانه نزدیک (خانه
) خودتان انجام 
 .دهید
شیوه هاتی برای بهبود بخشیدن فهم شما از زبان وجود دارد. برای مثال، اگر شما یم خواهید زبان انگلییس 
 تان یا هر زبان خاریحی دیگری را بهبود
 از این
 
ببخشید، باید بعض توصیه 
 .ها را در نظر بگیر ید اگر

 اضافه کنید، مطمئنأ تا حدودی
پیشر فت خواهید داشت.

newspapers are useful to improve your English.

society / despite / regions / mistake / specific / communication / suggestion / impossible / abilities 
1. It was a ----------- --------------- for us to come here tonight. 
2. I know a lot of children with different --------------------------. 
3. There was very little -------------------------- between the two brothers. 
4. They didn’t like my -------------------------- that we should all share the cost. 
5. He could eat a big lunch -------------------------- having eaten a big breakfast. 
6. Finding somewhere cheap to live in the city center is --------------------------. 
7. We must also consider the needs of the younger members of ----------- ---------------. 
8. We can find some endangered animals in the northern -------------------------- of our country. 
exist / decided / range / communicate / suggested / disappearing / varied / imagine / choose 
9. We can now -------------------------- easily with people on the other side of the world. 
10. Parents can -------------------------- the schools that they want their children to go to. 
11. My friend ----------- --------------- a Chinese restaurant near the station for the party. 
12. The scientists are sure that life does not -------------------------- on that planet. 
13. -------------------------- that you’re eating ice cream. Try to feel how cold it is. 
14. The cost of food and drink -------------------------- from week to week. 
15. These flowers are ----------- --------------- from our fields and woods. 
16. They -------------------------- to move abroad and make a fresh start.

 ). کلمات زیر را با تعاریف داده شده مطابقت دهید. (یک کلمه اضاق  یم باشد 
sign language / foreigner / synonym / make up / specific / range / despite / ability / parking lot 
1. Relating to one thing and not others:                                                                                  (--------------) 
2. To form a thing, amount or number:                                                                                  
 
(--------------) 
3. A person who comes from another country:                                                                       (--------------) 
4. The physical or mental power or skill to do something:                                                     (--------------) 
5. To have an upper and a lower limit in amount, number, etc. :                                            (--------------) 
6. An outside area of ground where you can leave a car for a period of time:                        (--------------) 
7. A word or phrase that has the same or nearly the same meaning as another word or phrase:        (--------------) 
8. A system of communication for people who cannot hear that uses hand and finger movements:  (--------------)

1. He tried to explain by ----------- --------------- of sign language. 
a. through
b. extra
c. means
d. plenty
 
2. We had a very hard winter last year and some of the plants --------------------------. 
a. escaped
b. died
c. varied
d. ranged
 
3. Does your brother play any other sports -------------------------- basketball? 
a. according to
b. plenty of
c. therefore
d. besides
 
4. Many of the world’s cities have -------------------------- of more than five million. 
a. mistakes
b. activities
c. objects
d. populations
 
5. What was his -------------------------- for why he was late? 
a. explanation
b. experience
c. suggestion
d. familiar
 
6. Any ----------- --------------- needs different services and all jobs are important and useful. 
a. institute
b. society
c. region
d. continent
 
7. When a person is --------------------------, he or she can speak a language easily, well, and quickly. 
a. mental
b. learner
c. fluent
d. familiar
 
8. You got twenty -------------------------- of the answers right. 
a. percent
b. interest
c. century
d. measure
 
9. We made some -------------------------- to the house before selling it. 
a. centuries
b. regions
c. interests
d. improvements
 
10. If you ----------- --------------- how long he’s been learning the piano, he’s not very good. 
a. consider
b. label
c. choose
d. communicate

-----, I don’t think your son will be successful in this field of study.

 آید.

کننده هاى قبل از اسم به کار می ر وند. (

 بصورت"مفرد "یم آید.) 
 :برای مثال1

c. much
d. little

d. much

4. زیر گزینه 
 .ی صحیح خط بکشید 
 
1. I am sorry, my (knowledge is / knowledge are) so poor that I can’t help you right now. 
2. We had very (little / a little) money left when we came back from holidays. 
3. The old man could not eat solids as he had very (a little / few) teeth left. 
4. I would like to make new friends. I’ve got (little / few) friends here. 
5. There is very (little / few) orange juice left in the fridge. 
6. How (many / much) plays did Shakespeare write? 
7. Eating too (many / much) chocolate is unhealthy. 
8. I can’t wait for you. I’ve got (few / little) time.

     A continent is a large area of the land on Earth that is ---(1)--- together. In general it is agreed   
there are seven ---(2)--- in the world: Africa, Antarctica, Asia, Europe, North America, Australia       
or Oceania, and South America. Some sources say that Australia is one of the ---(3)--- continents. 
Others say that Australia is part of Oceania. Oceania is a ---(4)--- which includes Australia, New 
Zealand and the Pacific Islands.  
     North America and South America are often ---(5)--- as “the Americans”. This has the 
advantage of including Central America and the Caribbean islands. Otherwise, Central America 
is counted as ---(6)--- of North America. 
1 a. appeared 
b. imagined 
c. joined 
d. scanned 
2 a. regions 
b. continents 
c. centuries 
d. societies 
3 a. five 
b. six 
c. seven 
d. eight 
4 a. part 
b. country 
c. institute 
d. region 
5 a. described 
b. escaped 
c. matched 
d. suggested 
6 a. part 
b. candle 
c. point 
d. percent

13. می   زیر را خوانده و به س ؤ( .االت به صورت خواسته شده پاسخ دهید3 
 )نمره 
      A sign language is a way of communicating by using the hands and other parts of the 
body. Sign languages are an important way for deaf people to communicate. Deaf people 
often use them instead of spoken languages.  
     Spoken languages use sounds from the mouth and are understood with the ears. Sign 
languages use hands and are understood with the eyes. Deaf people can use sign languages 
more easily than spoken languages. Deaf people sometimes learn a sign language from 
their family, especially if their parents are deaf. But most deaf children have hearing 
parents, so they learn a sign language from other deaf people. They may meet other deaf 
people at school or in the streets. Hearing people may learn to sign directly from deaf 
people. Or, they may learn a sign language by going to signing classes or by studying a 
sign language workbook, which can come with an interactive DVD. 
 
1. How do deaf people learn a sign language? 
    1.  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------. 
 
2. Which group of people often use sign languages? 
    2.  ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------. 
 
3. Hearing people cannot learn a sign language.                                                   (True or False) 
4. Sign languages are not important for deaf people.                                            (True or False) 
5. Sign languages use hands and other parts of the body.                                     (True or False) 
6. Hearing people can use spoken languages more easily than sign languages.   (True or False)

“Having a Healthier and Longer Life”   
"داشی   یک زندگی سالمی  و طوالب  تر"
 
     Have you ever thought of a healthy lifestyle to live longer? People can do many things to have a 
healthier life. Most people have a special diet or do lots of exercise; however, without a careful plan they 
may hurt themselves. 
    آیا تا کنون به یک 
 سبک زندگ  سالم برای  طوالت 
  تر زندگ 
 کردن فکر کرده اید؟ مردم یم توانند چیر های زیادی انجام دهند تا زندگ  سالم  
 تری

 .ها ممکن است به خودشان صدمه بزنند 
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------
--------------------
------------------------------------------------------
-----------------
-------------------------------------
 
     To have a healthier lifestyle, people need to do certain things. First, they should check their general 
health. Measuring blood pressure and heartbeat is the most important thing to do. They also need to 
check their family health history. In this way, they understand if anyone in the family has had a special 
illness. 
    برای داشئ   یک سبک زندگ  سالم تر، مردم باید کارهای خاض را انجام دهند. ابتدا، آنها باید سالمن  عمویم شان را چک کنند. اندازه 
 گیر ی
    بان قلب مهم ترین چیر

 فهمند که آیا هیچ( کیس در خانواده بیماری خاض داشته است
یا نه .)
 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
--------------------
------------------------------------------------------
-----------------
--------------------------------
-----
 
    Another thing is paying attention to physical health. For example, eating healthy food helps people 
live longer and prevents diseases. Eating junk food makes people gain weight, and increases the risk of 
heart attack. Eating balanced servings of bread, vegetables, fruits, protein, and oil is necessary for 
everyone. Also, daily exercises improve people’s health condition. 
 مطلب دیگر ، توجه کردن به سالمت جسیم است. برای مثال، خوردن غذای سالم به مردم کمک یم

 های

-----------------
-------------------------------------
 
 
   An effective way to enjoy a better lifestyle is having healthy relationships with others. Recent research 
has shown that a good social life decreases the risk of death. Sadly, some people do not visit their relatives 
very often these days. They are really busy with their work and usually use technology to communicate. 
 یک روش مؤثر برای لذت بردن از سبک زندگ  بهی  ، داشئ   روابط سالم با دیگران است. تحقیق اخیر نشان داده است که زندگ  اجتمایع

   Bad habits and addiction can be harmful to health. One day of smoking can take around 5 hours away 
from the smoker’s life. Addiction to technology such as using computers for a long time is also 
dangerous.

Reading (Vision Two - Lesson Two)

     “The Positive and Negative Effects of the Modern lifestyle”  
"تأثیر ات مثبت و منف  شیوه زندگی امروزی"
  
 
ج 
     
 
    The modern lifestyle has had both positive and negative effects on people’s lives. Modern 
technologies have enabled us to have easy access to information, become more creative, experience fast 
communication, travel easier, and have a more comfortable life. Have you ever imagined a world without 
the Internet, tablets, mobile phones, airplanes, and vacuum cleaners? Most people cannot do that, as 
technology is the miracle of our time. 
 
      شیوه 
  زندگ مدرن ، هم تأثیر ات مثبت و
هم تأثیر ا ت منف  بر روی زن  دگات 
 افراد داشته است. فن آوری

 آوری
معجزه 
 ی عرص ما یم  باشد.
 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
     But using new technologies has changed people’s lifestyle in a harmful way in this century as well. 
Some technologies are dangerous to our health and can harm our body. Using mobile phones or surfing 
the Internet for long hours can increase people’s blood pressure and cause sleep problems. Playing video 
games for long hours makes people nervous and harm their heart and nervous system. Listening to music 
by headsets can be harmful to one’s hearing and even brain . 
 
    
 اما استفاده از
تکنولوژی

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-----
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
     Using technology in a wrong way has created bad habits and new types of addictions. It is not strange 
now to call someone an Internet or mobile addict. Technology addicts are people with serious problems 
to control themselves to use various kinds of technology, in particular the Internet, smartphones, tablets 
and laptops. Technology addicts do not like to socialize with people; instead, they prefer to be alone and 
spend lots of their time working with their devices. This makes them depressed and impatient.

  می

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
--------------------
---------------------------------------------------------
-----------------
-------------------------------------
 
     Specialists have found different ways to cure technology addicts. To avoid this type of addiction, 
people should spend more time with their friends and family members, do daily exercise, and limit the 
time of working with technologies. They also need to enjoy nature more and have regular plans to travel.

      a. prevents                          b. rewrites                           c. discusses                       d. predicts 
   
17. I’m going on a -------------------- next week and I hope to lose three kilos before summer. 
      a. habit                                b. heartbeat                        c. addiction                      d. diet 
 
 
18. Her -------------------- isn’t good with her father, but she’s very close to her mother.  
      a. emotion                           b. relationship                    c. serving                          d. habit 
   
 
19. Because she had high --------------------, she had to prevent eating salty food. 
      a. heart attack                    b. addiction                         c. blood pressure             d. creation 
 
20. He always watches a lot of television and does nothing. He is a   ------------------. 
      a. couch potato                   b. writer                              c. researcher                    d. translator

 .. زیر گزینه ی صحیح خط بکشید 
1. Reza (went / has gone) to the cinema with Ali yesterday. 
2. She (have bought / has bought) two dresses, shoes and a handbag. 
3. He (took / have taken) the plane last week to go to Japan. 
4. Look at the dress I (has bought / have bought). Isn’t it beautiful? 
5. He (will eat / has eaten) too much chocolate and he feels sick. 
6. A: How long (do you live / have you lived) in this apartment?   B: About five years. 
7. My father (is / has been) in Yazd right now. He (is / has been) there for two weeks. 
8. She (didn’t see / hasn’t seen) her son for a long time. 
9. My brother (hasn’t called / didn’t call) me since Friday. 
10. Recently, we (had / have had) many parties with our friends. 
11. Have you ever (are / been) to England? 
12. When the egg was cooked she (ate / has eaten) it.

 ). شکل صحیح افعال داخل پرانی   را بنویسید. (حال کامل یا گذشته ی ساده 
1. We ----------------------------------------- (not play) football yesterday. 
2. I ----------------------------------------- (lose) my keys. I can’t find them. 
3. No, he ----------------------------------------- (not answer) yet. He is a lazy boy. 
4. Ahmad is late; I hope he ----------------------------------------- (not have) an accident. 
5. My uncle travels a lot. He ----------------------------------------- (visit) many countries. 
6. Yesterday, my brother ----------------------------------------- (repair) the car and washed it. 
7. Last Monday the teacher ----------------------------------------- (ask) us to check our exercises. 
8. I live in Tehran now, but I ----------------------------------------- (live) in Rasht for many years. 
9. My friend is a painter. He ----------------------------------------- (win) many prizes for his paintings. 
10. During the last summer holidays, I ----------------------------------------- (spend) one week in Turkey. 
11. My father is sixty years old and he ----------------------------------------- (never drive) a car in his whole life. 
12. Snow -------------------------------- (prevent) us from travelling since last Friday. We can’t go anywhere.

4
 . بهی  ین گزینه را .انتخاب کنید 
 
1. Ahmad works in a factory now, but he -------------------------------- a lot of different jobs. 
a. has
b. have had
c. had
d. has had
 
2. He’s only 30 and he -------------------------------- on all the seas and oceans of the world! 
a. has sailed
b. sails
c. have sailed
d. sailed
 
3. I -------------------------------- smoking several years ago. 
a. have given up
b. gave up
c. has given up
d. gives up
 
4. She -------------------------------- talking since the beginning of the lesson. 
a. doesn’t stop
b. hasn’t stopped
c. didn’t stop
d. haven’t stopped
 
5. When I was a child, I -------------------------------- to be a photographer. 
a. wanted
b. have wanted
c. want
d. has wanted
 
6. I -------------------------------- anything since breakfast. 
a. didn’t eat
b. hasn’t eaten
c. never eat
d. haven’t eaten
 
7. A: Why -------------------------------- this morning?     B: The traffic was bad. 
a. were you late
b. have you been
c. are you late
d. has you been
 
8. Have you ever -------------------------------- to Paris or to London? 
a. were
b. be
c. been
d. are
 
9. How many times ----------------------- Brazil ------------------------- the World Cup? 
a. does / win
b. has / won
c. did / win
d. have / won
 
10. A: Who is that man?     B: I don’t know. I -------------------------------- him before. 
a. has never seen
b. never see
c. have never seen
d. didn’t see
 
11. A: Is that a new coat?     B: No, I -------------------------------- this coat for a long time. 
a. have
b. have had
c. had
d. has had
 
12. I was doing my homework when my mother -------------------------------- home. 
a. comes back
b. has come back
c. came back
d. have come back
 
13. You ------------------- never ------------------ this lesson so you have to do more exercises.  
a. don’t / learn
b. didn’t / learn
c. has / learnt
d. have / learnt
 
14. He has -------------------------------- for 3 hours in this swimming pool.  
a. swim
b. swam
c. swum
d. swimming
 
15. I bought a new tie last week, but I  -------------------------------- it yet. 
a. haven’t worn
b. didn’t wear
c. hasn’t worn
d. don’t wear
 
 
16. A: Have you ever spoken to him.     B: Yes, I -------------------------------- to him last month. 
a. has spoken
b. have spoken
c. speak
d. spoke
 
17. He -------------------------------- his homework yet, although he started it two hours ago. 
a. didn’t finish
b. hasn’t finished
c. never finishes
d. haven’t finished
 
18. While I was walking in the park yesterday, I ------------------ a woman running very fast. 
a. saw
b. have seen
c. see
d. has seen
 
19. Sarah has lost her passport again. This is the second time this --------------------------------. 
a. happened
b. has happened
c. happens
d. have happened
 
20. My son -------------------------------- his English homework yet. 
a. didn’t do
b. haven’t done
c. doesn’t do
d. hasn’t done

 آید و خود آن
.حرف اضافه هم دیگر هنگام ترجمه نخواهد آمد و معنی ندارد

 صورت ضمیر باشد، فقط "مابیر  فعل و حرف اضافه" ب ه

      a. have ever seen              b. ever saw                             c. was ever going to see   d. will ever see

      a. had taken                      b. took                                     c. will be taking                d. have taken

      a. have just eaten             b. just ate                               c. will just eat                   d. had just eaten

      a. wrote / moved            b. have written / have moved c. have written / moved  d. wrote / have moved

      a. want / have called       b. wanted / called                  c. wanted / have called   d. have wanted / called

      a. go                                   b. to go                                    c. going                              d. from going

      a. do                                   b. doing of                              c. to do                              d. doing

      a. lived                               b. living                                  c. to live                            d. be living

       a. Eating / are                   b. Ate / is                                    c. Eating / is                     d. Ate / are

      a. eat / is                            b. eating / are                          c. eat / are                          d. eating / is

      a. taking / hike                  b. take / hike                           c. taking / hiking              d. took / hiking

     Addiction is when the body or mind ---(1)--- wants or needs something in order to work 
right. When you have addiction to something it is ---(2)--- being addicted or being an 
addict. People can be addicted to drugs, cigarettes, alcohol, caffeine, and many other 
things. When somebody is addicted to something, they can become ---(3)--- if they do not 
get the thing they are addicted to.  
     But taking more of the thing they are addicted to can also ---(4)--- their health. Some 
people who are addicts need to go to a doctor or hospital to ---(5)--- the addiction. People 
can also be addicted to other things which are not drugs. You may hear people talk about 
being addicted to the internet or addicted to chocolate or coffee. This means that they get 
into a ---(6)--- of enjoying that thing, so if they have to go without it for a while, they miss 
it a lot. 
 
1
 
a. well
b. slowly
c. badly
d. happily
2
 
a. avoided
b. called
c. taken
d. thought

b. excellent
c. sick
d. effective

b. enable

d. contain

14. می   زیر را خوانده و به س ؤ( .االت به صورت خواسته شده پاسخ دهید3 
 )نمره 
     
     A healthy diet or balanced diet is a diet that contains the right amounts of all the food 
groups. It includes fruit, vegetables, grains, dairy products, and protein. It does not include 
too much or too little of any kind of food. Eating wrong amounts of a food group, whether 
it is too much or too little, is called an “unhealthy diet” or an “imbalanced diet”.                        
    A food pyramid is a chart that can be used to see how many servings of each food should 
be eaten each day. It’s for having good health. Grains give carbohydrates and some 
vitamins and minerals. Vegetables and fruits give a lot of vitamins, some minerals, and few 
fats, but fruits often have more calories and sugar. Dairy products (like milk, cheese,

yogurt, etc.) have protein, some fat, and a lot of calcium, an element that helps build strong 
bones. Meat has protein, some fat, and B vitamins. Fats, oils, and sweets give calories, fats, 
and sugars. 
 
1. Fruits often have more calories and sugar than vegetables. ( True or False ) 
2. Protein is an element that helps build strong bones. ( True or False ) 
3. A healthy diet and a balanced diet are the same. ( True or False ) 
4. An imbalanced diet is good for our bodies. ( True or False )

Reading (Vision Two - Lesson Three) 
(Student Book: Pages 90 - 91)      
“Art, Culture and Society”     
"
 هنـر ،
 فرهنـگ و
جامعـه "
 
  
   
   
 
     
     Art is what people create with imagination and skill. As a part of culture, it shows the way of life and 
identity of a nation and reﬂects the history of a society. In fact, the history of humankind is the history 
of art. If we want to know a country or a nation well, we should study its art. 
هی  چیر ی است که افراد با تخیل و مهارت خلق یم 
  از فرهنگ، روش زندگ  و هویت ملت را نشان یم دهد و تاری  خ
ر
کنند. آن، به عنوان بخیس
یک جامعه را منعکس یم 
 کند. در حقیقت، تاری  خ بشر یت تاری  خ هی  است. اگر ما بخواهیم یک کشور یا یک ملت را به خوتی بشانسیم، باید
 .هی  آن را مطالعه کنیم 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
    Handicrafts are good examples of the art and culture of a country. By handicrafts, we mean making 
decorative items in a skillful way using our hands. Each country and culture has its own handicrafts. 
     صنایع دسن  مثال 
  ماهرانه با استفاده
هاتی خوب از هی  و فرهنگ یک کشور هستند منظور از صنایع دسن  ، ساخئ   اشیاء تزئین  به رویسر
از دست 
 هایمان .است. هر کشور و فرهنگ صنایع دسن  خودش را دارد 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
     Making and selling handicrafts are good ways to help a country’s economy and introduce its culture 
to other nations. Many people of the world produce handicrafts and sell them to tourists. In some Asiana 
countries a part of the country’s income comes from making and selling handicrafts. 
 ساخئ   و
فروخئ   صنایع دسن  روش  های خوتی برای کمک کردن به اقتصاد کشور و معرف  کردن فرهنگش به ملت 
 .های دیگر هستند
بسیاری از مردم دنیا صنایع دسن  تولید یم 
 کنند و آنها را به گردشگر ها یم 
  از درآمد یک کشور
ر

فروشند. در بعض
از درست کردن و فروخئ   صنا(  یع دسن
به دست
) یم  
 .آید 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
    Iran has a five-thousand-year-old history of artistic works and handicrafts including pottery, painting, 
calligraphy, rugs and carpets, etc. If you travel across Iran, you’ll get back home with excellent 
handicrafts as souvenirs for your family and friends. 
 ایران تاری  خ پنج هزار ساله از آثار هی  ی و صنایع دسن  شامل س ، خطایط، قالیچه، فرش و غیر ه دارد. اگر در شاش ایران سفر
ر
فال، نقایس
کنید، با 
 .صنایع دسن  عال به عنوان سوغات  برای خانواده و دوستانتان به خانه بر یم گردید 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
     Iranian art is also quite famous all around the world. There are very excellent collections of Persian 
art in many important museums of the world. If we want to name countries with richest art and cultural 
diversity, Iran is among them. Persian art is famous in the world for reﬂecting moral and social values 
of Iranian people and the natural beauty of this vast country. 
 هی  ایرات  هم چنئر  در شتاش دنیا کامأل معروف است. در تعداد زیادی از موزه های مهم دنیا کلکسیون 
 های خییل عال از هی  ایرات  وجود
دارد. اگر بخواهیم کشورهاتی با غن  ترین تنوع هی  و فرهنگ را نام بیی یم ، ایران از جمله ی آن 
 ها است. هی  ایران به خاطر منعکس کردن ارزش  
 .های معنوی و اجتمایع مردم ایران و زیباتی طبییع این کشور پهناور، در دنیا معروف است 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-----------
--------------------------
 
 
     Iranian craftsmen and craftswomen are famous for producing very unique artworks from wood, metal 
and other simple materials around them. Many people of the world appreciate the art and skill of a young 
Iranian girl who weaves a beautiful silk carpet in a small village of Azarbaijan or Kordistan. When 
tourists buy Persian rugs or carpets, they take a part of Iranian art and culture to their homelands. 
   
 استادکاران مرد و زن ایرات  به خاطر تولید کردن آثار هی  ی خییل منحرص به
فرد از چوب، فلز و مواد ساده 
 ی دیگر در دور و اطرافشان، معروف
 .هستند

کردستان یم بافد، را یم دانند. وقن  گردشگرها قالیچه یا فرش های ایرات  را یم

Reading (Vision Two - Lesson Three) 
(Workbook: Pages 46 - 47 
“Cultures and Lifestyles”                  
"فرهنگ ها و سبک 
 های زندگی"
 
  
    
 
    
     Culture is a system of shared beliefs that are common in a society. Often, we think of the food, 
music, clothing, and holidays that are common in a society as its culture, but these are only some 
of the elements. Other  elements include customs, values, behaviors, and artifacts. Culture is, 
therefore, a combination of thoughts, feelings, attitudes, and beliefs. 
 
   
 فرهنگ ، 
 سیستیم از عقاید به اشی  اک گذاشته شده است که در یک جامعه
متداول 
 .هستند اغلب ،  ، ما غذا، موسیف پوشاک و
  تعطیالت (مراسمات)

رایج

) در نظر یم  
،گیر یم 
 اما اینها تنها برای 
  بریح 
 از اجزا 
 .هستند سایر اجزا 
 شامل سنتها، ارزشها، 
 رفتارها و  صنایع دسن 
 .هستند 
 ،بنابراین 
 ،فرهنگ ترکینی از افکار
احساسات
، نگرش 
 ها و
 عقاید
می 
 باشد .
 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
   With more than 190 countries and 7 billion people on earth, it is not hard to imagine that many 
cultures exist. No matter where you go around the world, you will face people, lifestyles and 
cultures that are different to what you have. People around the world have very different lives 
and ways of living. They have different beliefs and customs. So, they usually live and behave 
according to what they believe to be right and wrong. 
 
    
 با بیش از۱۹0
  
 کشور و۷ 
 میلیارد انسان بر  روی کره  زمئر  ، تصور کردن اینکه فرهنگ 
 های
زیادی

 دارای سبک 
 های زندگ  و سنتهاتی

 به  درسن 
 و غلط بودن آن ،دارند زندگ  و رفتار یم 
 .کنند 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
     Learning to respect other cultures is important for having new experiences and learning about 
the world. One of the first steps to learn about other cultures is to simply accept that there are 
many different cultures exist other than our own culture. One of the most important ways to learn 
to become respectful of other cultures is to spend some time reflecting on our own. Then it is 
important to understand something about other cultures.

(بدانیم) .
 
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
-------------------------------------
 
    For those who want to learn about other cultures, but do not know where to start, a great place 
to start is reading about the cultures that interest them. In today’s world if we all are able to know 
about other cultures, and respect them, life would be easier for most of us.

     a. vast                            b. unique                       c. former                       d. moral 
4. How much you’re paid ----------------- how important you are to the company you work for. 
     a. introduces                 b. recognizes                 c. attempts                    d. reflects  
5. Our strong sense of national ------------------- has been shaped by our history. 
     a. essay                          b. fingerprint                c. identity                      d. product  
6. Tom seemed -------------------- by the whole situation that occurred for the family in that novel.  
     a. satisfied                     b. cheerful                    c. amused                       d. shared  
7. His back was ------------------- under the weight of the heavy box that he lifted. 
     a. pressed                      b. bowed                       c. hared                         d. depended  
8. We need to rethink the way we consume energy. Take, ----------------- our approach to transport. 
     a. frightened to death  b. how touching            c. for instance               d. well-done  
9. Every Christmas we have a collection of people for a local -------------------. 
     a. souvenir                    b. custom                       c. identity                      d. charity  
10. A ------------------- of factors may be responsible for the increase in cancer. 
     a. product                     b. pack                           c. charity                       d. combination   
11.  You'll need a variety of skills, ---------------- leadership and negotiating with your personnel. 
     a. touching                    b. bowing                      c. including                   d. attempting  
12. I think you can run a business to the highest ------------------- standards in the society. 
     a. pressing                     b. moral                        c. well-done                   d. former  
13. The world Health Organization has ------------------- alcoholism as a disease since 1951. 
     a. recognized                b. satisfied                     c. shared                        d. introduced  
14. The article made no ------------------- to previous researches on the subject. 
     a. reference                   b. introduction             c. essay                           d. identity  
15. Terrorism is ------- ---------- unacceptable in a civilized world and every government faces it severely. 
     a. totally                        b. appreciately              c. formerly                     d. repeatedly  
16. The curtains are for purely ------------------- purposes and do not open or close. 
     a. touching                    b. cheerful                     c. decorative                 d. pressing  
17. Customers are offered a 10% ------------------- if they pay cash for buying decorative items. 
     a. product                     b. reference                   c. discount                     d. identity  
18. Does television adequately reflect the ethnic and cultural ------------------- of the country? 
     a. collection                   b. identity                     c. custom                       d. diversity

     a. craftsmen                  b. artistic                       c. art work                    d. calligraphic

7. If you -------------------------------- (not slow) down, you will have an accident. 
8. If the questions -------------------------------- (be) easy enough, everyone will pass the test. 
9. My boss -------------------------------- (make) a good decision if he has all the information. 
10. If everything -------------------------------- (work) out like I think, the business will be a great success.

 Culture is a word for the way of life of a group of people, meaning the way they do things. Different 
groups may have different cultures. Culture is seen in people’s writing, religion, music, clothes, cooking, 
and in what they do. Cultures are what make countries ---1---. Each country has different cultural ---2---. 
Culture includes material goods, the things the people use and ---3---.

14
( .. می   زیر را خوانده و به سئواالت به صورت خواسته شده پاسخ دهید3 
 )نمره 
      
      Photography is a way of making a picture using a camera. A person who makes pictures using a camera 
is called a photographer. A picture made using a camera is called a photograph or photo. A camera is, in 
the simplest form, like a box with a hole in front of it. There is a special piece of glass in front of the hole 
called a lens. To take a photograph of something, the lens makes a small picture of the object inside the 
camera. The lens does this by focusing light.  
      A lens in camera works like a lens in glasses. To make a photograph with a camera the shutter release 
button is pressed. Pressing the button opens the shutter. The shutter is like a door. It covers the hole in the 
camera box. When the shutter is open light can enter the camera. When the button is pressed the shutter 
opens and then closes. This happens very fast. The amount of time the shutter stays open for is called the 
shutter speed. The shutter speed can change between 1/1000th of a second to a few seconds. Normally the 
time taken for the shutter to open and close is far less than one second. 
1. A picture made using a camera is called a photo. (True or False) 
2. The shutter covers the hole in the camera box. (True or False) 
3. A lens in camera doesn’t work like a lens in glasses. (True or False) 
4. A video camera is a device that takes pictures. (True or False) 
 
5. What does a photographer do?         
    5. -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------. 
 
6. What is the shutter speed?                
    6. -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------.

 .. می   زیر را خوانده و به سؤاالت به صورت خواسته شده پاسخ دهید(
5
 /
1
 )نمره 
 
     Social media are different from traditional media. In the past, media were created from one 
place, normally a company. Then the company would send out the media to people. Magazines, 
books and movies are all examples of this. But with social media, many people in different places 
create content. Another difference is that people who create content in social media do not need 
very special skills. On the other hand, in traditional media, people need special skills to create 
something.                            
     Another difference is that social media can be created much faster. It takes only a few minutes 
to write a twitter post, for example. But it can take many months to put together a magazine, or 
years to make a movie. Some people think social media are good and useful and some think they 
are bad. No matter what you might think, social media are here to stay and millions of people use 
them every day. 
 
34. Magazines are types of social media.                              (True or False) 
35. You need special skills to create traditional media.         (True or False) 
36. How many people use social media?

      a. crafts                        b. items                          c. carpets                         d. poems

      a. keeping on                b. referring to              c. searching for                d. being familiar 
 
5- To find the size, length, or amount of something, using standard units such as meters etc. is ------------.         
   a. broadcast                  b. measure                   c. access                            d. predict

      a. run out of                                                         b. be familiar with        
      c. take care of                                                       d. meet the needs of

to live together is called ---------------------. 
      a. society                       b. population                c. communication            d. region

زبان انگلــییس 
 پایه یازدهم"        
       
 
            “Vision Two”
 تألیـــف و گـــردآوری :
"جمـــــال احــــمـدی و همــــکاران" 
 
 
  
English to English Dictionary (Lesson 1 - Vision 2) 
1. ability / əˈbɪləti / n. the power and knowledge to do something 
    She has the ability to pass the exam, but she must work harder. 
 
2. absolutely / ˈæbsəluːtli / adv. completely 
    It’s absolutely freezing outside! 
 
3. access / ˈækses / n. the right to enter a place, use sth, see sb etc 
   All students have access to the library. 
 
4. across / əˈkrɒs / adv. from one side to the other side of sth 
   The river was about twenty meter across. 
 
5. actually / ˈæktʃuəli / adv. really; in fact 
   You don’t actually believe her, do you? 
 
6. additional / əˈdɪʃənəl / adj. more than what was agreed or expected 
    Additional information can be obtained from the center. 
 
7. afraid / əˈfreɪd / adj. frightened; scared 
     I’m afraid of wild animals. 
\8. amazing / əˈmeɪzɪŋ / adj. surprising 
    I’ve got an amazing story to tell you. 
 
9. appear / əˈpɪr / verb. used when saying how someone or something seems 
    He appears to be strong and healthy. 
 
10. appropriate / əˈprəʊpri-ət / adj suitable or right for a particular situation, person, etc. 
    That question isn’t appropriate. 
 
11. beginner / bɪˈɡɪnər / n. a person just starting to learn a skill or take part in an activity, starter 
    This is a great book for the beginners. 
 
12. belief / bəˈliːf / n. a sure feeling that something is true or real 
    My belief is that he will succeed. 
 
13. borrow / ˈbɒrəʊ / v. to take and use something that you will give back after a short time 
    Can I borrow your pen? 
 
14. bring / brɒːt / v. to take something or sb with you to a place 
    Could you bring me a glass of water? 
 
15. by means of / baɪ miːnz ɑːv / prep. With the help of; by using 
    The blocks are raised by means of pulleys. 
 
16. candle / ˈkændl / n. a stick of wax with a string through the middle, which you burn to give light 
   My mother put thirteen candles on my birthday cake. 
 
17. capital / ˈkæpətl / n. the most important city in a country, where the government is. 
   Tokyo is the capital of Japan. 
18. century / ˈsentʃəri / n. a period of 100 years 
    A century is one hundred years.

            “Vision Two”
 تألیـــف و گـــردآوری :
"جمـــــال احــــمـدی و همــــکاران" 
 
 
  
19. certainly / ˈsɜːtnli / adv. without any doubt 
   Tom certainly wasn’t too happy last night. 
 
20. choose / tʃuːz / v. to decide which thing or person you want 
    Choose your next words carefully. 
 
21. circle / ˈsɜːkəl / n. v. a round shape; a ring 
     There are 360 degrees in a circle. 
 
22. column / ˈkɒləm / n. a tall solid piece of stone that supports part of a building. 
     Each page of this dictionary has two columns. 
 
23. communicate / kəˈmjuːnɪkeɪt / v. to share and exchange information, ideas or feelings with sb 
     We use words in order to communicate. 
 
24. compare / kəmˈpeə $ -ˈper / v. to consider two or more things or people, in order to show how they 
are similar or different 
     They compared the new car with the old one. 
 
25. complete / kəmˈpliːt / v. used to emphasize that a quality or situation is as great as it could be 
     They asked me to complete the forms. 
 
26. confident / ˈkɒnfɪdənt / adj. sure that sth will happen in the way that you want or expect 
    He is confident that he will pass the examination. 
 
27. consider / kənˈsɪdə $ -ər / v. to think about sth carefully, especially before making a choice or decision 
     Have you considered trying to find a job in Boston? 
 
28. continent / ˈkɒntɪnənt / n. a large mass of land surrounded by sea 
    Africa is the poorest continent. 
 
 
29. countable / ˈkaʊntəbəl / adj. a countable noun has both a singular and a plural form 
    Nouns like car, book, … are countable. 
 
30. culture / ˈkʌltʃə $ -ər / n. the customs, ideas and way of life of a group of people or a country 
    In many cultures, men and women dress differently. 
 
31. despite / dɪˈspaɪt / prep. used to say that sth happens or is true although sth else might have prevented it 
    We decided to go out despite the bad weather. 
 
32. develop / dɪˈveləp / v. to grow slowly, increase, or change into sth else; 
    Students should develop their reading skills. 
 
33. die / daɪ / v. to stop living 
    We all die sooner or later. 
 
34. disappear / ˌdɪsəˈpɪə $ -ˈpɪr / v. to become impossible to see any longer 
    The sun disappeared behind the clouds. 
 
35. escape / ɪˈskeɪp / v. to get away from a dangerous or bad situation 
     I didn’t help Tom escape. 
 
 
36. exchange / ɪksˈtʃeɪndʒ / v. giving or receiving sth in return for sth else 
    We exchanged phone numbers. 
 
37. exist / ɪɡˈzɪst / v. to be real; to live 
    Animals cannot exist without air and water. 
 
38. experience / ɪkˈspɪəriəns / n. knowing about sth because you have seen it or done it 
    Taking care of animals is a valuable experience.

39. extra / ˈekstrə / adj, adv more than what is usual 
    John has three extra tickets he wants to sell. 
 
40. familiar / fəˈmɪliə $ -ər / adj. that you know well 
     I heard a familiar voice in the next room. 
 
41. ﬂuently / ˈﬂuːəntli / adv. able to speak easily and correctly 
    She speaks five languages ﬂuently. 
 
42. follow / ˈfɑːloʊ / v. to come or go after sb or something 
    Some students find it diffcult to follow the rules. 
 
43. following / ˈfɒləʊɪŋ / adj. next 
     Please correct the following sentence. 
 
44. foreign / ˈfɒrɪn / adj. from or relating to a country that is not your own 
    There are many foreign students in Iran. 
 
45. forest / ˈfɒrɪst / n. a large area of land covered with trees 
    We went for a walk in the forest. 
 
46. fortunately / ˈfɔːtʃənətli / adv. luckily 
    Fortunately we arrived on time. 
 
47. happen / ˈhæpən / v. to take place, usually without being planned first 
    How did the accident happen? 
 
48. hard-working / hɑːrd ˈwɜːkɪŋ / adj. working with a lot of effort 
    I saw a hard-working teacher. 
 
49. hint / hɪnt / n. v. advice, suggestion, mention, sign 
    My father hinted that it was time to go to bed. 
 
50. history / history / n. all the things that happened in the past 
    I want to teach history when I grow up. 
 
 
51. honest / ˈɒnɪst / adj. A person who is honest says what is true and does not steal, lie or cheat 
    She’s a very honest person. 
 
52. idea / aɪˈdɪə / n. a suggestion for a possible course of action, especially one that you think of suddenly 
    It was my wife’s idea to move house. 
 
 
53. imagine / ɪˈmædʒɪn / v. to make a picture of sth in your mind 
     I tried to imagine life on the moon. 
 
54. imaging / ˈɪmɪdʒɪŋ / n. the process of producing an exact picture of sth, especially on a  screen 
     New imaging technologies mean that doctors are better able to screen for breast cancer. 
 
55. improve / ɪmˈpruːv / v. to make sth better, or to become better 
     You could use the money for improving your home. 
 
56. improvement / ɪmˈpruːvmənt / n. the act of improving sth, or the state of being improved 
    He has made a big improvement in tennis. 
 
57. institute / ˈɪnstɪtjuːt / n. a group of people who meet to study or talk about a special thing; the 
building where they meet 
    I’m studying in the Institute of Science.

58. interestingly / ˈɪntrəstɪŋli / adv. a fact that you think is interesting

59. international / ˌɪntəˈnæʃənəl / adj. between different countries 
      English is an international language. 
 
60. interview / ˈɪntəvju / n. v. a formal meeting at which someone is asking you some questions 
    The interview began at 10 o’clock. 
 
61. invite / ɪnˈvaɪtɪŋ / v. to ask sb to come to a party, to your house, etc 
    Anna invited me to her party. 
 
62. juice / dʒuːs / n. the liquid from fruit and vegetables 
    What kind of fruit juice do you like? 
 
63. keep off / kiːp ɑːv / v. to prevent sth from touching or harming sth 
     The government should keep its hands off content. 
 
64. knowledge / ˈnɒlɪdʒ / n. what you know and understand about sth

                                      * write information on sth 
      Read what’s on the label.                              Label the goods clearly. 
 
66. lake / leɪk / n. a big area of water with land all around it 
    We went swimming in the lake. 
 
67. laugh / lɑːf / v. to make sounds to show that you are happy or that you think sth is funny 
    His jokes always make me laugh. 
 
68. live / laɪv / adj. adv. living, having life 
    “They will show the game live on television.” 
 
69. loaf / ləʊf / adj. a quantity of bread that is shaped and baked in one piece 
    I bought a loaf of bread for breakfast. 
 
70. look for / lʊk fɔːr / v. search

71. lose / luːz / v. unable to find sth. 
    I can’t open the door because I’ve lost my key. 
 
72. luckily / ˈlʌkəli / adv. it is lucky that 
    I was late, but luckily they waited for me. 
 
73. make up / meɪk ʌp / n. special powders and creams that you put on face to look more beautiful 
    She put on her make-up. 
 
74. manner / ˈmænə $ -ər / n. the way that you do sth or the way that sth happens 
    I‘m here to teach you some manners. 
 
75. matter / ˈmætə $ -ər / n. sth that you must talk about or do. 
    No matter what happens, I won’t give up.

89. period / ˈpɪəriəd / n. an amount of time

90. photograph / ˈfəʊtəɡrɑːf / n. a picture that you take with a camera 
    He took a photograph of the hotel. 
 
91. percent / pɚˈsent / adv. for or out of every 100 
     We have a five percent chance of success. 
 
92. pick / pɪk / v. to take the person or thing you like best 
    Peter picked a ﬂower and smelled it. 
93. point / pɔɪnt / n. a fact, an idea or an opinion 
    You made some interesting points in your essay. 
 
94. popular / ˈpɒpjələ / adj. liked by a lot of people 
    Football is a popular sport in Britain.

110. respect / rɪˈspekt / n. thinking that sb or sth is very good or clever

111. scientist / ˈsaɪəntɪst / n. a person who studies science or works with science

112. seek / siːk / v. to try to find or get sth

113. sign / saɪn / n. sth that tells you that sth exists, is happening or may happen in the future 
      Dark clouds are a sign of rain. 
 
114. similar / ˈsɪmɪlə $ -ər / adj. the same in some ways but not completely the same 
     Jane and her sister look very similar. 
 
115. simply / ˈsɪmpli / adv. a word that you use when you want to show how easy or basic sth is 
      These questions can be answered quite simply. 
 
116. sing / sɪŋ / v. to make music with your voice 
     The birds were singing. 
 
117. singular / ˈsɪŋɡjələ $ -ər / n. the form of a word that you use for one person or thing 
     The singular of ‘men’ is ‘man’. 
 
118. skill / skɪl / n. the ability to do sth well 
      Students should develop their reading skills. 
 
119. smart / smɑːrt / adj. clever 
      She’s not as smart as her sister. 
 
120. smile / smaɪl / v. to move your mouth to show that you are happy or that you think sth is funny 
     He smiled at me. 
 
121. society / səˈsaɪəti / n. a large group of people who live in the same country or area and have the 
same ideas about how to live 
     A family is the smallest unit of society. 
 
122. song / sɒːŋ / n. a piece of music with words that you sing 
     The story is told through song and dance. 
 
123. space / speɪs / n. a place that is big enough for sb or sth to go into or onto 
     Is there space for me in your car? 
 
124. specifc / spəˈsɪfɪk / adj. exact and clear 
     He gave us specifc instructions on how to get there. 
 
125. step / step / n. a movement when you move your foot up and put it down in another place to     
walk, run or dance 
      She took a step forward and then stopped. 
 
126. suggest / səˈdʒest / v. to tell sb your ideas about what they should do, where they should go etc. 
      I suggest you study French. 
 
127. suitable / ˈsuːtəbəl / adj. right for sb or sth 
      This flm isn’t suitable for children. 
128. surfing / ˈsɜːfɪŋ / n. the sport of riding on waves while standing on a surfboard 
      His hobbies include surfing and photography. 
 
129. surprise / səˈpraɪz / n. feeling that you have when sth happens suddenly that you did not expect 
      He looked up in surprise when I walked in. 
 
130. swim / swɪm / v. to move your body through water 
       I swim across the lake.

131. tiny / ˈtaɪni / adj. very small 
      Ants are tiny insects. 
 
132. tongue / tʌŋ / n. the soft part inside your mouth that moves when you talk or eat 
     His name is on the tip of my tongue. 
 
133. translator / rænsˈleɪtə / n. someone who changes writing into a different language 
      She works as a translator. 
 
134. turn off / tɜːrn ɒːf / v. to stop the ﬂow of electricity, water, ect. By moving a switch, tap ect. 
      Don’t forget to turn the lights off when you leave. 
 
135. turn on / tɜːrn ɒːn / v. to start the ﬂow of electricity, water, ect. By moving a switch, tap ect. 
      He turned on the lights when he entered the room. 
 
136. twice / twaɪs / adv. two times 
      I have been to Japan twice. 
 
137. uncountable / ʌnˈkaʊntəbəl / adj. nouns are ones that have no plural. 
      After an uncountable number of hours, they were both tired. 
 
138. underline / ˌʌndəˈlaɪn / v. to draw a line under a word or words 
      Correct the underlined words. 
 
139. understand / ˌʌndəˈstænd / v. to know what sth means or why sth happens 
          He doesn’t understand Spanish. 
 
140. understanding / ˌʌndəˈstændɪŋ / n. knowledge about sth, based on learning or experience 
      Success depends on a clear understanding of the problem. 
 
141. useful / ˈjuːsfəl / adj. what is usable and helpful 
      Learning French is useful. 
 
142. valuable / ˈvæljuəbəl / adj. worth a lot of money 
      Taking care of animals is a valuable experience. 
 
143. value / ˈvæljuː / n. v. how much money you can sell sth for 
       A healthy man does not know the value of health. 
 
144. vary / ˈveəri / v. to be different from each other or to change according to the situation 
     The price varies according to the quality. 
 
145. visitor / ˈvɪzɪtə / n. a person who goes to see another person or a place for a short time 
      The old lady never has any visitors. 
 
146. weather / ˈweðə $ -ər / n. how much sun, rain or wind there is at a certain time 
     We had bad weather last week. 
 
147. weekend / ˌwiːkˈend / n. the end of the week 
      Friday is the weekend in Iran. 
 
148. wish / wɪʃ / v. to want sth that is not possible or that will probably not happen 
      I wish I passed the exam!

English to English Dictionary (Lesson 2 - Vision 2) 
1. accident / ˈæksɪdənt / n. an event in which a car, train etc. is damaged and often sb is hurt 
    Her sister was killed in a car accident. You’re responsible for this. 
 
2. activity / ækˈtɪvəti / n. sth that you do because you enjoy it 
    She takes part in many school activities. 
 
3. actually / ˈæktʃuəli / adv. used to add new information to what you have just said 
     I actually don’t work here. 
 
4. addict / ˈædɪkt / n. someone who is unable to stop taking drugs 
    His uncle is an addict. 
 
5. addiction / əˈdɪkʃən / n. the need to take a harmful drug regularly, without being able to stop 
    Drug addiction is a cancer in modern society. 
 
6. affirmative / əˈfɜːmətɪv / n. an affirmative answer or action means ‘yes’ or shows agreement 
    He accepted her reply as an affirmative. 
  
7. against / əˈɡenst / prep. used to say that sb opposes or disagrees with sth 
     I was vaccinated against the ﬂu. 
 
8. antibiotic / ˌæntɪbaɪˈɒtɪk / n. a drug that is used to kill bacteria and cure infections 
     Antibiotics are a cure for infection. 
 
9. ahead / əˈhed / adv. a short distance in front of someone or sth 
    We have a busy day ahead of us. 
 
10. attend / əˈtend / v. to go to an event such as a meeting or a class 
      I will not attend the party. 
 
11. avoid / əˈvɔɪd / v. to prevent sth bad from happening 
      If you drive carefully, you’ll avoid accidents. 
 
12. balance / ˈbæləns / n. a state in which all your weight is evenly spread so that you do not fall 
      I lost my balance and fell on my face. 
 
13. balanced / ˈbælənst / adj. giving equal attention to all sides or opinions 
      Most of us don’t eat a balanced diet. 
 
14. behave / bɪˈheɪv / v. to act in a specified way 
     You should try to behave better. 
 
15. bear / beə / v. bravely accept or deal with a painful, difficult situation 
      I can’t bear the pain anymore. 
 
16. busy / ˈbɪzi / adj. if you are busy, you are working hard and have a lot of things to do 
      She’s busy now – can you phone later? 
 
 
17. calm / kɑːm / adj. relaxed and quiet, not angry, nervous, or upset 
      We must keep calm. 
 
18. care / keə / n. the process of looking after sb, especially because they are ill, old, or very young 
      high standards of medical care

19. carry / ˈkæri / v. to hold sth in your hand or arms, or support it as you take it somewhere 
    Gina was carrying a small bunch of ﬂowers. 
 
20. cause / kɔːz / v. to make sth happen, especially sth bad 
    Heavy traffc is causing delays on the freeway. 
 
21. change / tʃeɪndʒ / v. to become different, or to make sth become different 
    The leaves on trees change colour in the autumn. 
 
22. comfortable / ˈkʌmftəbəl / adj. feeling physically relaxed, without any pain etc. 
    Sit down and make yourself comfortable. 
 
23. common / ˈkɒmən / adj. existing in large numbers 
     Daisies are very common ﬂowers. 
 
24. communicate / kəˈmjuːnɪkeɪt / v. to exchange information or conversation with other people 
     We communicated mostly by e-mail. 
 
25. compare / kəmˈpeə / v. to be better or worse than sth else 
     Let’s compare the translation with the original. 
 
26. condition / kənˈdɪʃən / n. how healthy or ft you are 
     I do not think he will accept our conditions. 
 
27. confuse / kənˈfjuːz / v. to make someone feel that they cannot think clearly or do not understand 
     I understand the text but the diagrams are confusing me. 
 
28. contain / kənˈteɪn / v. if sth such as a bag, box, or place contains sth, that thing is inside it 
     The thieves stole a purse containing banknotes. 
 
29. couch / kaʊtʃ / n. a comfortable piece of furniture big enough for two or three people 
     Tom offered to sleep on the couch. 
 
30. create / kriˈeɪt / v. to make sth exist that did not exist before 
     Her behaviour is creating a lot of problems. 
 
31. creative / kriˈeɪtɪv / adj. involving the use of imagination to produce new ideas or things 
     Carlos is really intelligent and creative. 
 
32. cultural / ˈkʌltʃərəl / adj. belonging or relating to a particular society and its way of life 
      I think that cultural exchanges are important. 
 
33. cure / kjʊə / n. a medicine or medical treatment that makes an illness go away 
      There is still no cure for AIDS. 
 
34. cycle / ˈsaɪkəl / v. to travel by bicycle 
      I plan to cycle around Shikoku next year. 
 
35. dangerous / ˈdeɪndʒərəs / adj. able or likely to harm or kill you 
     It’s dangerous to play around the fire. 
 
36. decrease / dɪˈkriːs / v. to become less or go down to a lower level, or to make sth do this 
     We want to decrease our reliance on oil. 
 
37. depress / dɪˈpres / v. to make someone feel very unhappy 
    The thought of taking the exam again depressed him.

38. depression / dɪˈpreʃən / n. a feeling of sadness that makes you think there is no hope  
     Mary is showing signs of severe depression. 
 
39. describe / dɪˈskraɪb / v. to say what sth or someone is like by giving details about them 
    The police asked her to describe the man. 
 
40. detail / ˈdiːteɪl / n. a single feature, fact, or piece of information about sth 
    She told me every detail of her trip. 
 
41. diet / ˈdaɪət / n. the kind of food that a person or animal eats each day 
    She doesn’t eat a very healthy diet. 
 
42. discuss / dɪˈskʌs / v. to talk about sth with another person or a group in order to exchange ideas  
    Littman refused to discuss the case publicly. 
 
43. disorder / dɪsˈɔːdə / n. a mental or physical illness which prevents body from working properly 
    The room was in total disorder. 
 
44. education / ˌedjʊˈkeɪʃən / n. the process of teaching and learning, usually at school, or university 
     Education is the key to success. 
 
45. affect / əˈfekt / v. to do sth that produces an effect or change in sth or in sb’s situation 
      A lack of sleep affected the singer’s performance. 
 
46. effective / ɪˈfektɪv / adj. successful, and working in the way that was intended 
      We need to find an effective method. 
 
47. emergency / ɪˈmɜːdʒənsi / n. a dangerous situation that must be dealt with immediately 
      There was an emergency on the road. 
 
48. emotion / ɪˈməʊʃən / n. a strong human feeling such as love, hate, or anger 
      Her voice was full of emotion. 
 
49. emotional / ɪˈməʊʃənəl / adj. relating to your feelings or how you control them 
     Mahdi has some emotional problems. 
 
50. especially / ɪˈspeʃəli / adv. used to emphasize that sth is more important 
     I never liked long walks, especially in winter. 
 
51. excellent / ˈeksələnt / adj. extremely good or of very high quality 
      Walking is an excellent exercise. 
 
52. exciting / ɪkˈsaɪtɪŋ / adj. making you feel excited 
      Last night’s game was exciting. 
 
53. exercise / ˈeksəsaɪz / n. physical activities that you do to stay healthy and become stronger 
      I always take some exercise before breakfast. 
 
54. experience / ɪkˈspɪəriəns / n. knowledge or skill that you gain from doing a job or activity  
      I’ve got more experience than John. 
55. famous / ˈfeɪməs / adj . known about by many people in many places 
      I have a friend whose father is a famous actor. 
 
56. favorite / ˈfeɪvərət / adj. your favorite person or thing is the one that you like the most 
     What’s your favorite activity?

60. frequency / ˈfriːkwənsi / n. the number of times that sth happens in a particular period of time 
    the frequency of serious road accidents             
   
61. future / ˈfjuːtʃə / n. likely to happen or exist at a time after the present 
    Who knows what will happen in the future? I’d like to be a dentist in the future. 
 
62. gain / ɡeɪn / v. to obtain or achieve sth you want or need 
     The company gained more customers. 
 
63. general / ˈdʒenərəl / adj. relating to only the main features or parts of sth, not the details

64. go away / ɡoʊ əˈweɪ / v. to leave a place or person

66. guess / ɡes / v. to try to answer a question or form an opinion when you are not sure  
      I’d say he’s around 50, but I’m only guessing. 
 
67. habit / ˈhæbɪt / n. a regular tendency or practice, especially one that is hard to give up 
     He is in the habit of reading the newspaper while eating. 
 
68. hang out / hæŋ aʊt / v. to spend a lot of time in a particular place or with particular people 
     I don’t like to hang out with people who smoke. 
 
69. harm / hɑːm / n. damage, injury, or trouble caused by someone’s actions or by an event 
     Smoking will do you a lot of harm.  
 
70. harmful / ˈhɑːmfəl / adj. causing harm  
      Smoking is harmful to your health. 
 
71. headset / ˈhedset / n. a set of headphones, often with a microphone attached 
    Please show me how to use the headset. 
 
72. health / helθ / n. the general condition of your body and how healthy you are 
    I’m worried about my husband’s health. 
 
73. heartbeat / ˈhɑːtbiːt / n. the action or sound of your heart as it pumps blood through your body 
    The doctor measured the patient’s heartbeat and blood pressure. 
 
74. hiking / ˈhaɪkɪŋ / n. the activity of taking long walks in the mountains or country 
    Tochal is a great place to go hiking.

89. interested in / ˈɪntrɪstɪd / adj. if you are interested in doing or having sth, you want to do or have it

120. prefer / prɪˈfɜː / v.to like sb or sth more than sb or sth else, so that you would choose it

121. prefix / ˈpriːfɪks / n. a letter or letters added to the beginning of a word to make a new word 
     In the word “unimportant”, “un-” is a prefix. 
 
122. prepare / prɪˈpeə / v. to make a meal or a substance 
      Prepare the sauce while the pasta is cooking. 
 
123. present / ˈprezənt / adj. happening or existing now 
     Foreign observers were present at the elections. 
 
124. press / pres / v. 1. to push sth firmly against a surface 
                                  2. to make clothes smooth using a hot iron 
     Can you tell me which button to press? 
     He pressed his pants. 
 
125. pressure / ˈpreʃə / n. a difficult situation that makes you feel worried or unhappy 
     I’ve been under a lot of pressure lately. 
 
126. prevent / prɪˈvent / v. to stop sth from happening, or stop sb from doing sth 
     The rules are intended to prevent accidents. 
 
127. proportion / prəˈpɔːʃən / n. a part of a number or an amount, considered in relation to whole 
     The proportion of women graduates has increased in recent years. 
 
128. price / praɪs / n. the amount of money you have to pay for sth 
     The price of fuel keeps going up. 
 
129. produce / prəˈdjuːs / v. to make, write etc. sth to be bought, used, or enjoyed by people 
     This factory produces CD players. 
 
130. progressive / prəˈɡresɪv / adj. continuous 
    ‘She is cleaning the yard’ has present progressive tense. 
 
131. properly / ˈprɒpəli / adv. correctly, or in a way that is considered right  
    The brakes don’t seem to be working properly.

132. positive / ˈpɒzətɪv / adj. good or useful 
     If you don’t have anything positive to say, don’t say anything. 
 
133. quince / kwɪns / n. a hard yellow fruit like a large apple, used in cooking

134. rarely / ˈreəli / adv. not often 
     She very rarely complains. 
 
135. recreation / ˌrekriˈeɪʃən / n. an activity that you do for pleasure or amusement 
     His only recreations are drinking beer and watching football. 
    There is no money to provide recreational facilities. adj. 
 
136. relationship / rɪˈleɪʃənʃɪp / n. the way 2 people or two groups feel and behave towards each other 
     I have quite a good relationship with my parents. 
 
137. replay / ˈriːpleɪ / v. to play back a recording on tape, video, or repeat sth, especially an event 
      He could stop the tape and replay it whenever he wished.’ 
 
138. retire / rɪˈtaɪə / v. to stop working, usually because you have reached a certain age  
      Most people retire at 65. 
 
139. risk / rɪsk / n. the possibility that sth bad, unpleasant, or dangerous may happen 
      Skiers always face the risk of serious injury. 
 
140. regular / ˈreɡjələ / adj. happening or doing sth very often 
      He paid regular amounts of money to a charity. 
 
141. rush / rʌʃ / v. quickly, especially because you need to be somewhere very soon 
     The firefighter rushed into the burning house. 
 
142. scientifc / ˌsaɪənˈtɪfɪk / adj. about or relating to science, or using its methods 
     He made an important scientifc discovery. 
 
143. series / ˈsɪəriːz / n. a group of events or actions that are planned to happen one after the other 
     She made a series of medical discoveries. 
 
144. serious / ˈsɪəriəs / adj. a serious situation, problem, accident etc. is extremely bad or dangerous 
      Luckily, the damage was not serious. 
 
145. serving / ˈsɜːvɪŋ / n. an amount of food that is enough for one person 
     You should prepare four servings for our guests. 
 
146. situation / ˌsɪtʃuˈeɪʃən / n. the set of things that are happening at a particular time and place 
      I explained the situation to everyone. 
 
147. skim / skɪm / v. to read sth quickly to find the main facts or ideas in it 
      Julie skimmed the sports page to find the main idea. 
 
148. smoking / ˈsməʊkɪŋ / n. the activity of breathing in tobacco smoke from a cigarette, pipe etc. 
      I gave up smoking nearly ten years ago. 
 
149. snack / snæk / n. a small amount of food that is eaten between main meals or instead of a meal 
      I had a snack before I went back to work. 
 
150. specialist / ˈspeʃəlɪst / n. sb who knows a lot about a particular subject, or is very skilled at it 
      We need great specialists for this job.

English to English Dictionary (Lesson 3 - Vision 2) 
 
1. accept / əkˈsept / v. to take sth that sb offers you, or to agree to do sth that sb asks you to do 
    Rick accepted her offer of coffee. 
 
2. activity / ækˈtɪvəti / n. a lot of things happening and people doing things 
   There were all sorts of group activities in our high school. 
 
3. actually / ˈæktʃuəli / adv. really; in fact 
    I can’t believe I’m actually going to Australia! 
 
4. according to / əˈkɔːding tuː / prep. as shown by sth or stated by someone 
   According to the police, his attackers beat him with a blunt instrument. 
 
5. add / ˌæd / v. to put sth with sth else 
   We like to add new members to our group. 
 
6. amazing / əˈmeɪzɪŋ / adj. It surprises you very much and is difficult to believe 
    He can do the most amazing acrobatics. 
 
7. antonym / ˈæntənɪm / n. a word that means the opposite of another word 
   Right is the antonym for left. 
 
8. appreciate / əˈpriːʃieɪt / v. to understand how important a situation or problem is or what sb’s feelings are 
   My boss doesn’t appreciate me. 
 
9. area / ˈeəriə / n. a part of a town, country or the world 
   Tom is going to stay in the Boston area. 
 
10. arrow / ˈærəʊ / n a long thin piece of wood or metal with a point at one end 
   The arrow is pointing right. 
 
11. art / ɑːt / n. the use of painting, drawing, sculpture etc. to represent things or express ideas 
    Weaving rugs is an art. 
 
12. artifact / ˈɑːtəfækt / n . an object such as a tool, weapon etc. that was made in the past and is 
historically important 
     I heard they found an alien artifact on the moon. 
 
13. artist / ˈɑːtɪst / n. a person who creates works of art 
    The poor man finally became a great artist. 
 
14. artwork / ˈɑːtwɜːk / n. paintings and other objects produced by artists 
I use five clear plastic frames to rotate my sons’ latest artwork. 
 
15. ashamed / əˈʃeɪmd / adj. feeling sorry and unhappy because you have done sth wrong, or because 
you are not as good as other people 
    I was ashamed about lying to my parents. 
 
16. attitude / ˈætɪtjuːd / n. the opinions and feelings that you usually have about sth, especially when 
this is shown in your behavior 
 
    The people have a very positive attitude to life.

24. behaviour / bɪˈheɪvjə $ -ər / n. the things that a person or animal does

26. bow / bəʊ / v. to bend the top part of your body forward in order to show respect for someone 
important, or as a way of thanking an audience

27. brain / breɪn / n. the part inside the head of a person or an animal that thinks and feels

28. calligraphy / kəˈlɪɡrəf / n. the art of producing beautiful writing using special pens or brushes, or the 
writing produced in this way 
    Chinese calligraphy is considered an art form. 
 
29. carpet / ˈkɑːpɪt / n. a soft covering for a ﬂoor that is often made of wool and is usually the same size 
as the ﬂoor 
    My bedroom carpet is green. 
 
30. charity / ˈtʃærəti / n. an organization that collects money to help people who need it 
   They give a lot of money to charity. 
 
31. custom / ˈkʌstəm / n. sth that is done by people in a particular society because it is traditional 
    This custom is dying out nowadays. 
    It’s a custom to celebrate Christmas. 
 
32. craft / krɑːft / n. a job or activity for which you need skill with your hands. 
    She learned the craft of basket weaving from her grandmother.

"جمـــــال احــــمـدی و همــــکاران" 
 
 
  
102. science / ˈsaɪəns / n. the study of natural things 
    Physics is a branch of science. 
 
103. share / ʃeər / v. to have or use sth with other people. 
    The three of us shared a taxi. 
 
104. shock / ʃɑːk / v. to give sb a very bad surprise; to upset sb 
    I was shocked by his behavior. 
 
105. show / ʃoʊ / v. to let sb see sth 
    Tom showed his photo album to Mary. 
 
106. simply / ˈsɪmpli / adv. used to emphasize what you are saying, easily 
     This work is simply not good enough. 
 
107. similar / ˈsɪmɪlə $ -ər / adj. almost the same 
    Your handwriting is similar to mine 
 
 
108. site / saɪt / n. a place where a building is, was, or will be 
     We visited the historical sites of the country. 
 
109. skill / skɪl / n. an ability to do sth well, especially because you have learned and practiced it. 
     Any jobs today require computer skills. 
 
110. skillful / ˈskɪlfəl / adj. very good at doing sth 
    She is a very skilful tennis player. 
 
111. sociable / ˈsəʊʃəbəl / adj. friendly and enjoying being with other people 
    His little sister is very sociable. 
 
112. society / səˈsaɪəti / n. a large group of people who live in the same country or area and have the 
same ideas about how to live 
    Some people say Japan is a male-dominated society. 
 
113. soft / sɒft / adj. not hard or firm 
    I just want to sleep in a nice soft bed. 
 
114. souvenir / ˌsuːvəˈnɪə / n. sth that you keep to remember a place or a special event 
   Are there any souvenir shops here? 
 
115. suffix / ˈsʌfɪks / n. a letter or letters added to the end of a word to form a new word, such as ‘ness’ 
in ‘kindness’ or ‘ly’ in ‘suddenly’ 
    ‘ous’ is an adjective making suffix. 
 
116. surprised / səˈpraɪzd / adj. having a feeling of surprise 
    I was surprised to hear the news. 
 
117. special / ˈspeʃəl / adj. not usual or ordinary; important for a reason 
    It’s my birthday today so we are having a special dinner. 
 
118. spend / spend / v. to use your money to pay for goods or services 
    I only want to spend about $20. 
    I spend my free time with my kids. 
 
119. store / stɔːr / n. a big shop that sells many different types of things 
   Harrods is a famous London store.
